    WF-1 — MRI example CLI/config: femem run study.yaml¶

    Item briefs and execution history (operator directive 2026-10-01). The §7 row is the status of record.

    §9 item 256 — 2026-10-05 (chain U5)¶

    Outcome: ✅ DONE 2026-10-06 00:41Z (19:30 CDT slot, attempt 1). Five windows green on first run; no in-slot fix. Execution record at the end of this file.

    Drafted by a general-purpose agent for the 2026-10-05 18:00 daily review; the review reviews it. Licence: §10 chain U5 (PROJECT_PLAN.md:4739–4741), a weekly-enumerated step (§9 rule (4)). It is serial on U4, and U4 is met: WF-16 ✅ (ee9be842), WF-15 ✅ (4239ba88). The WF-1 §7 row (PROJECT_PLAN.md:2876) has no Done-when, and the 10-03 weekly left it for the daily to write ("the daily writes the Done-when at rubric grain"). The Done-when under "Status it can move" below is that text. The review copies it into the row in its own commit (rule (j)). No band, tolerance, record or validation target moves. Every band below is imported by name from the file:line cited, and none is set here.

    WF-1 step 1: a thin YAML layer over Study, and a femem console script. femem run <file>.yaml parses a YAML study file into the same Study object that WF-15 / WF-16 build in Python. It solves the study and writes the outputs: a JSON summary, plus a Touchstone file for a port study. - Two committed files drive the two gated fixtures at degree 1, -n 2, to the records those chunks already assert. - Three malformed files fail with a named field. - The installed console script refuses the same three. - Executor: implementer. - What it adds: - two new src/ modules (YAML loader, CLI) plus a __main__.py; - one additive [project.scripts] table in pyproject.toml; - three __all__ exports; - two new test modules and seven YAML files; - the registry and snapshot entries. - What it does not touch: study.py, any existing gate module, helper or record. - Tier: W1 standard (180 s); W2 heavy by ceiling (400 s, the ceiling WF-16 W1 had on the same fixture at the same width); W3 / W4 the WF-14 / WF-13 windows verbatim; W5 smoke (installed console script, real build). - Ranks: -n 2 for every solve window, which is the width of both records. Complex build for W1 / W2. - Class: chain step (solve, degree 1 only). Measured median ≈ 9 min (the 10-05 restock table). - Cost: ≈ 50 min predicted: - 15 fixed + ≈ 25 code; - windows ≈ 1.7 + 2.5 + 0.1 + 0.6 + 0.5 min; - ≈ 5 spare. - Depends on: nothing open. Nothing named cli, femem, load_study or StudyConfigError exists in src/. pyproject.toml has no [project.scripts] (read 2026-10-05). pyyaml is both a declared dependency (pyproject.toml) and installed in the image (docker/Dockerfile:15). - Shared files. Items 259 / 260 may also edit src/fem_em_solver/__init__.py, tests/package/public_surface.txt and tests/package/validation_envelope.toml. Make every change there by edit, adding lines only. On a rebase, keep both sides (both sets of names, both sets of tables). This item does not edit study.py, so 259 / 260's study.py edits cannot conflict with it.

    Settled from the code (read before designing). - The Python-built comparands, imported and not rebuilt by hand: - Sphere: tests/package/test_wf15_study_lossy_sphere.py, _study(degree=1) (:111–119) on GEOMETRY (:98–104). That is σ 0.57 / εᵣ 78 (_material_057, :107), QuasiStaticSphereField(e0=E0), frequency_hz=FREQUENCY_HZ and density_kg_m3=RHO_KG_M3. - Birdcage: Study(FourPortBirdcageGeometry.f_small(), GelledSalinePhantomMaterial(SALINE_SIGMA, SALINE_EPSILON_R, FREQUENCY_64_HZ), LumpedPortDrive(), frequency_hz=FREQUENCY_64_HZ, degree=1), which is test_wf16_study_four_port.py:139–145. - The records and bands: - Sphere: MAT4_DEG1_RECORD 8.291558128e-08 and MAT4_DEG1_PRINT "8.291558e-08" (test_wf15…:87–88), L0_BAND 1e-9 (test_mat5_em_coupled_sphere.py:104), and MAT4_BAND (test_phantom_material_model.py:222). - Birdcage: ANS4_EXACT_STRIP_S_P1_RECORDS["64"] / ANS4_EXACT_STRIP_S_P2_X1["64"] and ANS4_EXACT_STRIP_RECORD_RANK_WIDTH (test_ans4_exact_strip_records.py), LEG_D0_REPRODUCTION_BAND (test_port_birdcage_four_port.py:165), ANCHOR_TOL / FREQ_ATOL_HZ (tests/io/test_touchstone_skrf_crosscheck.py:51–52), and the PORT-11 bands and helpers exactly as test_wf16…:73–89 imports them. - What WF-15 / WF-16 measured on these comparisons today: - Sphere, -n 2, degree 1: (I) Study against direct, in-process, 0.000e+00; (R1) 6.222e-11; (R2) the string matched (20261005T170210Z_WF-15-r2-W1-deg1.log). - Birdcage, -n 2, degree 1: (R1) 9.557e-11 against P1; (T1) 6.873e-14; (T2) 9.557e-11; (I) in-process 9.110e-16; (c1) cross-degree 0.0491; (c2) P1↔P2 swap 0.2492 (20261005T202623Z_WF-16-W1-deg1.log). - Degree 2 at -n 8 (20261005T203015Z_WF-16-W2-deg2.log) is not re-run here (Scope). - "Bit-identical" cannot mean bitwise solver output. WF-16's in-process Study-against-direct read 9.110e-16, not 0. -n 2 MUMPS is also not bitwise across processes (known-issues.md:69, OPS-43; SOLVE_FLOOR_RECORD at known-issues.md:81). The reading is WF-15's rule-(j) reading (PROJECT_PLAN.md:2890): - bitwise up to the solve: the YAML-built Study's spec equals the Python-built one field by field, with floats compared by float.hex, and the DG0 material fields assembled from it are equal; - the solve outputs within the imported bands: SAR at L0_BAND; S and the Touchstone file at LEG_D0_REPRODUCTION_BAND; - plus the registered records the U3 / U4 tests already assert.

    The Done-when states this as "(read by the 2026-10-05 18:00 review, rule (j); the weekly ratifies)". - The JSON round trip is exact. json.dump writes a Python float by repr, and json.load reads it back to the same double. So the summary file's SAR and S equal the in-memory values bitwise, and the test may compare the file's numbers with == where it says so. - PyYAML's float resolver (YAML 1.1) needs a dot and a signed exponent. Measured on the host's PyYAML 6.0.1, yaml.safe_load gives: - 64.0e6 → str and 1e-9 → str; - 6.4e+07, 64000000.0 and 0.5700000000000001 → float; - 78 → int and true → bool; - for a: 1\na: 2 → {'a': 2}: the duplicate key is dropped silently.

    The container's pyyaml is unpinned, so the test prints yaml.__version__. - The literals the good files carry. The files write each float as its Python repr: - repr(0.01 / 10.0) is 0.001 and repr(0.01 / 5.0) is 0.002, both == the decimal literals (host arithmetic, 2026-10-05); - repr(64.0e6) is 64000000.0; - repr(np.nextafter(0.57, 1)) is 0.5700000000000001.

    (K) checks every bit, so a hand-typed literal that is one ULP off fails there, in the parse node, before any solve. - The material frequency is the study's. GelledSalinePhantomMaterial(sigma, epsilon_r, frequency_hz, mu_r=1.0) is a frozen dataclass (materials/phantom.py:21–40). The YAML material section has no frequency key: the loader passes study.frequency_hz. A mismatch would raise at core/time_harmonic.py:462–465, and that is not one of the three gated failures. - femem will not be on PATH in the dev container. The image's pip install -e . (docker/Dockerfile:36) predates the entry point, so in-container windows call fem_em_solver.cli.main([...]) in-process. The console script is exercised only in WF-13's fresh venv. That venv's pip install --no-deps --no-build-isolation . (scripts/package/wf13_setup.sh:84) builds a wheel from the copied pyproject.toml and does install [project.scripts]. Never pip install into the live container.

    Code. - (1) New src/fem_em_solver/study_yaml.py with three public names: - StudyConfigError(ValueError), with attributes .field (the dotted path, e.g. "geometry.sphere_raduis") and .source (the file path). Its message is f"{source}: {field}: {reason}", so the field name appears in str(exc). - load_study(path) -> Study. It parses with a yaml.SafeLoader subclass whose construct_mapping raises StudyConfigError on a duplicate key. Never use yaml.load. import yaml happens inside the function, so import fem_em_solver does not import yaml. - run_study_file(path, *, output_dir=None, comm=MPI.COMM_WORLD) -> StudyRun. It calls load_study, then study.solve(comm), then writes the outputs. StudyRun (study, result, summary dict, written paths) is a dataclass and is not exported, as PortStudyResult was not. - Schema v0. Units are SI and given by the key name; a unit string is a type error. - femem (int, must be 1; required). - study: frequency_hz (number, required), degree (int in {1, 2}, optional, default 2, the Study's own default), density_kg_m3 (number, optional, default the Study's). - geometry: kind (required). - sphere_in_box: sphere_radius, box_half_width, resolution_sphere, resolution_far (numbers, required), sphere_tag (int, optional). - four_port_birdcage: preset: f_small (optional), plus any FourPortBirdcageGeometry field name as an override. A list is converted to a tuple for port_box_size. Any other key, including width_correction_kappa, is an unknown key. - material: either preset (low / mid / high, which goes through Study's own string route) or sigma + epsilon_r (+ optional mu_r), building GelledSalinePhantomMaterial(sigma, epsilon_r, study.frequency_hz, mu_r). Both forms at once is an error on material. - excitation: kind (required). - quasi_static_sphere_field: e0 (optional). - lumped_port_drive: z0_ohm, port_impedance_ohm (optional numbers), drive_voltage_v (optional: a number or a [re, im] pair). - outputs (optional): directory. - Numbers. A number field accepts int or float, converted by float(). It rejects bool (it is an int subclass) and str. The str message names the YAML-1.1 trap: "YAML reads 64.0e6 as a string; write 6.4e+07 or 64000000.0". - Validation order is fixed, and the first error raises: - at top level: the version, then unknown top-level keys, then missing required sections; - then section by section in the order study → geometry → material → excitation → outputs; - within a section: unknown keys, then missing required keys, then types and values. - No solver code, no mesh before the parse completes, and no import from tests/. A StudyConfigError is raised identically on every rank, because every rank parses the same file. There is no collective before it. - Outputs. run_study_file resolves output_dir as the argument, else outputs.directory, else ./femem_out/<stem>. After every reduction, rank 0 creates it and writes summary.json. Then comm.Barrier(), and every rank returns. The summary holds: - femem 1, source (absolute path), sha256 of the file, family, degree, frequency_hz, ranks, cells, elapsed_s; - sphere: the mean_sar dict with every value float()-ed; - ports: s_matrix and z_matrix as nested [re, im] pairs, and touchstone (the filename).

    For a port study it calls `result.write_touchstone(<output_dir>/<stem>.s4p)` (`study.py:405–421`, rank 0 plus `Barrier`).
    • (2) New src/fem_em_solver/cli.py. It defines main(argv=None) -> int with argparse subcommands:
    • run <file> [--output-dir DIR] calls run_study_file and returns 0;
    • check <file> calls load_study only and prints, on rank 0, one line per spec field (family, degree, frequency, the material's σ / εᵣ, the excitation kind). It returns 0.

    Behaviour: - A StudyConfigError prints femem: error: <message> to stderr on rank 0 and returns 2 on every rank. - main returns an exit code and never calls sys.exit. The console-script wrapper does that. - (3) New src/fem_em_solver/__main__.py: import sys; from .cli import main; sys.exit(main()). It is not exercised by a window, so it is disclosed as unexercised. - (4) pyproject.toml: add one table, [project.scripts] with femem = "fem_em_solver.cli:main". Nothing else changes. - (5) src/fem_em_solver/__init__.py: import StudyConfigError, load_study and run_study_file from .study_yaml, and append them to __all__ (30 → 33). cli is not imported by __init__. - (6) tests/package/public_surface.txt: add the three names, keeping the sort order: StudyConfigError after Study, load_study before load_touchstone, run_study_file after run_single_port_excitation_case. - (7) tests/package/validation_envelope.toml: - add [names.StudyConfigError] (the malformed node and the installed node), [names.load_study] (the good-file and malformed nodes) and [names.run_study_file] (the two CLI nodes); - append test_wf1_cli_sphere_reproduces_wf15 to [names.Study]; - node IDs only, no number (WF-14 (N)). - (8) Seven YAML files under tests/package/wf1/: - sphere_mat4.yaml: femem: 1; study: {frequency_hz: 64000000.0, degree: 1, density_kg_m3: 1000.0}; geometry: {kind: sphere_in_box, sphere_radius: 0.01, box_half_width: 0.02, resolution_sphere: 0.001, resolution_far: 0.002, sphere_tag: 1}; material: {sigma: 0.57, epsilon_r: 78.0}; excitation: {kind: quasi_static_sphere_field, e0: 1.0}. - f_small_four_port.yaml: femem: 1; study: {frequency_hz: 64000000.0, degree: 1}; geometry: {kind: four_port_birdcage, preset: f_small}; material: {sigma: 0.5, epsilon_r: 78.0}; excitation: {kind: lumped_port_drive}. - control_sigma_ulp.yaml: sphere_mat4.yaml with sigma: 0.5700000000000001 and no other change. - malformed_unknown_key.yaml: sphere_mat4.yaml with sphere_radius misspelled sphere_raduis. Expected .field = geometry.sphere_raduis. - malformed_wrong_type.yaml: sphere_mat4.yaml with frequency_hz: 64.0e6, which YAML reads as a str. Expected .field = study.frequency_hz. - malformed_missing_section.yaml: f_small_four_port.yaml with no excitation: section. Expected .field = excitation. - malformed_duplicate_key.yaml: sphere_mat4.yaml with sigma given twice (0.57, then 0.75). Expected .field = material.sigma. This is the loader's guard, printed and asserted in the parse node, and not one of the row's three.

    Each file has a one-line comment header citing this item. Each malformed file differs from its parent in exactly the one stated place. - (9) New tests/package/test_wf1_study_yaml.py. @complex_only goes on the two solve nodes, so WF-14's real-build collection imports the module cleanly. - Imports from test_wf15… / test_wf16…: helpers and constants only, namely _study, GEOMETRY, _closed, _record, _rel and the constants. Never a test_* function or a fixture (deg1, leg): an imported test name is collected again in this module. - Helper _spec_diff(a, b) -> list[str]. It returns the dotted names that differ between two Study objects. It walks geometry, material and excitation dataclass fields plus frequency_hz, degree and density_kg_m3. It compares type() first, then floats by float.hex, complex numbers by the hex of their real and imaginary parts, tuples elementwise, and everything else by ==. - Nodes: - test_wf1_good_files_build_the_python_studies (no solve; (K), (Deg), (c1) parse half, duplicate key); - test_wf1_malformed_files_name_their_field (no solve; (X)); - test_wf1_cli_sphere_reproduces_wf15 (W1); - test_wf1_cli_f_small_reproduces_wf16 (W2). - Width guard on both solve nodes, asserted and never skipped: MPI.COMM_WORLD.size == ANS4_EXACT_STRIP_RECORD_RANK_WIDTH (2). - Output paths. Each CLI node first has rank 0 delete /workspace/logs/wf1/<stem>/ if it exists, then Barrier; /logs/ is gitignored, .gitignore:82. It then calls cli.main(["run", <file>, "--output-dir", "/workspace/logs/wf1/<stem>"]) in-process and reads summary.json on every rank after main returns. A stale summary therefore cannot pass for a fresh one. - (10) New tests/package/test_wf1_installed_cli.py. It uses stdlib only (subprocess, sys, pathlib), with no import from tests/ and none of fem_em_solver. It is skipped unless WF13_INSTALLED_CHECK=1, and it locates femem at Path(sys.prefix) / "bin" / "femem". Node: test_wf1_installed_femem_refuses_the_malformed_files (W5, (X′)).

    Anchors (asserted). - (K) The YAML builds the Python Study, bitwise and field by field (no solve). - _spec_diff(load_study("sphere_mat4.yaml"), _study(degree=1)) == []. - _spec_diff(load_study("f_small_four_port.yaml"), <the WF-16 Python Study at degree=1>) == []. - The preset route: the sphere mapping with material: {preset: mid}, built through the private mapping entry point that load_study uses (no extra file), gives _spec_diff(..., _study(material="mid", degree=1)) == []. - Print each list, and the float.hex of geometry.resolution_sphere / resolution_far / study.frequency_hz on both sides. - (Deg) Degree 2 when omitted, structurally. Both good mappings with study.degree deleted give .degree == 2. Exact. - (X) Three malformed files fail with a named field (no solve). For each malformed file: - load_study raises StudyConfigError, not a bare KeyError, TypeError or YAML error; - exc.field == the expected dotted path above, and that path in str(exc); - cli.main(["run", <file>, "--output-dir", "/workspace/logs/wf1/malformed"]) returns 2 on every rank (allreduced MPI.MIN == MPI.MAX == 2), and the output directory does not exist afterwards, which shows the parse fails before any mesh or write. - The three .field values are pairwise distinct. - Duplicate key (printed and asserted, beside the three): malformed_duplicate_key.yaml raises StudyConfigError with .field == "material.sigma". - (U) Upstream identity through the CLI's own parse, sphere (W1). - DG0 σ / εᵣ / μᵣ come from build_material_fields / build_mu_r_field, built on the Python Study's solved mesh / cell_tags once with load_study(file).material as phantom_material and once with _study(degree=1).material, both with phantom_tag=SPHERE_TAG and the same background. - Each reads max |Δ| == 0.0 with 0 differing on owned dofs, using _dg0_owned_diff (test_phantom_material_model.py, as WF-15 imports it). - (S) The CLI's outputs are the run's. In both CLI nodes: - rc == 0 on every rank; - summary.json exists, with femem == 1, degree == 1, ranks == 2, and family equal to the file's kind; - cells == the Python-built Study's global cell count in the same process, exactly; the version-tagged 74 020 / 117 206 are printed and never asserted at equality. - (R) The records, through the CLI. - Sphere: (R1) |SAR_yaml − MAT4_DEG1_RECORD| / record ≤ L0_BAND, where SAR_yaml is the summary's mean_sar_w_per_kg; (R2) f"{SAR_yaml:.6e}" == MAT4_DEG1_PRINT.     Execution record — item 256 (2026-10-06, 19:30 CDT slot)¶

    Landed as briefed. src/fem_em_solver/study_yaml.py (StudyConfigError, load_study, run_study_file, the private _study_from_mapping, the non-exported StudyRun), src/fem_em_solver/cli.py (main, run / check), src/fem_em_solver/__main__.py, [project.scripts] femem = "fem_em_solver.cli:main", three __all__ names (30 → 33), the snapshot and registry entries, seven YAML files under tests/package/wf1/, and two test modules. study.py is untouched.

    Two implementation choices to disclose. - Duplicate-key guard. The loader is a yaml.SafeLoader subclass whose construct_mapping refuses a repeated key. To name the field by its dotted path, it composes the node tree first, then constructs it, and maps the offending mapping node back to its path. It reads material.sigma on the guard file. - Order inside geometry / excitation. kind is checked first (missing, then unknown value), because the allowed key set depends on it. Unknown keys, missing keys and types follow in the briefed order. A geometry.kind / excitation.kind mismatch (a sphere driven by ports, or the reverse) is refused as excitation.kind. None of the three gated files reaches either branch.

The Python Study refuses the same combination later, at its first solve-bearing call: _conductor_setup raises ValueError matching "as_hole" before it touches built (item 388 (K): sar, b1_plus, solve() on a {"mesh": None} stub, 20261012T033238Z_WF-16-s11-W1.log, 12 passed).     Review ruling 2026-10-14 03:00 (item 442)¶

    The brief's two questions are ruled: - Schema. tune: is a top-level YAML section, as U46's thermal: is. It is carried inside the parsed outputs dict so _load's four-tuple (more than 12 callers) does not grow. - Wrong-fixture control. Dropping it is accepted. The k = 2 control (item 429's measured +48.95 %) and the refusals are enough. - Bitwise scope. The bitwise check is against the same Study's tune() only. Across two builds the bar is LEG_D0_REPRODUCTION_BAND (rule (j) of the brief). - Re-gates. RG1 / RG2 are owed in the slot (standing rule (c)), as are the registry pages.

    Review ruling 2026-10-19 03:00 (item 545, U67)¶

    • Rule (6) count: U65, U66 and U67 are chain U's three provisional steps before the 10-19 weekly (the cap). The weekly ratifies or reverts all three. No band, tolerance, Done-when or target is set or moved.
    • Q1, the two pinned rows: as proposed. Move both inputs to outputs: {probes: []}: 500's row at tests/package/test_wf1_yaml_thermal_ball.py:317–318, and item 539's (c-ball) at tests/package/test_wf1_yaml_thermal_box_probes.py:217–222, with its print text and docstring. W0 asserts that both still raise with field outputs.probes. These are the only existing assertions this item edits, and both edits are disclosed in the journal. This is the 18:00 pattern for 521b's row.
    • Q2: R1 stands. The ball path calls _parse_box_probes unedited. Calling an existing parser is neither a copy nor a factor, and one parser is the smaller surface.
    • Q3: refuse the walled ball (the narrower admission).     Review ruling 2026-11-05 03:00 — U125 is step 28; the walled box's public keys¶

    The 11-04 weekly wrote U125 as "WF-1 step 10". That number is stale: step 10 is used, and the latest step is 27 (item 730). U125 is step 28.

    Reproducing P63's R1 "on the same mesh" needs two public-surface choices. Both are written here as execution detail, with the target kept, and named in the dashboard for the 11-07 weekly to ratify or revert (as field_strength_t was).

    1. thermal.initial placement and the wall keys. - thermal.initial sits under thermal: as a sibling of transient, so _TOP_KEYS is unchanged. - geometry.wall uses the ball's _WALL_KEYS names (thickness_m, k_w_per_m_k, rho_c_j_per_m3_k), plus divisions. - divisions is the number of cells across the wall. P63 uses N_w = 2 (test_mat21_wall_fem.py:74–75), and it cannot be derived from the other keys.

    2. A walled box is P48b's 1D-reduced form. - The wall sits on ±z only. - Robin cooling applies on z = ±(C + d) only. - The four side faces are Neumann (test_mat21_wall_fem.py:12–15; P63's _robin_facets(..., (2,))).

    "The test's own record". P63's W1b prints only R1 − R0 at P5, not θ_R1 itself. So the new module computes P63's R1 directly in the same window and compares against that, and it checks the drift against W1b's print.
